### Execution, dogfood and external-effect records

| Record / schema | Exact canonical fields and predicates |
|---|---|
| `ExecutionIdentityV1` / `execution-identity-v1` | `kind,schema_version,repository_identity:identity,worker_identity:dbIdentity,host_identity:dbIdentity,runtime_owner_sha256:sha256<RuntimeOwnerIdentityV1>,working_directory_sha256:sha256<PathIdentityV1>,argv_sha256:sha256<StringListV1>,environment_policy_sha256:sha256<EnvironmentPolicyV1>,executable_sha256:blob256,toolchain_sha256:sha256<ToolchainIdentityV1>,materialized_resource_profile_id:id,materialized_resource_profile_sha256:sha256<MaterializedResourceProfileV1>,secret_capabilities:[]secret_capability(set,1..4),codex_provider_isolation_profile_sha256?:sha256<CodexProviderIsolationProfileV1>,ralphex_sha256?:sha256<ToolIdentityV1>,codex_sha256?:sha256<ToolIdentityV1>,codex_model?:id,codex_effort?:id`; runtime worker/host equal the owner record; `P-RESOURCE-003` requires the secret set and complete direct profile to equal the materialized profile. |
| `ToolIdentityV1` / `tool-identity-v1` | `kind,schema_version,tool_id,name,version,source,source_revision,executable_sha256:blob256,executor,provider_id?:id,model?:id,reasoning_effort?:id` |
| `CodexProviderIsolationProfileV1` / `codex-provider-isolation-profile-v1` | `kind,schema_version,id="CODEX_BROKER_V1",provider_id="abcp-broker",wire_api="responses",base_url_template="http://127.0.0.1:{reserved-port}/v1",allowed_routes:["POST /responses","POST /responses/compact"],requires_openai_auth=false,env_key_name="ABCP_BROKER_SESSION_TOKEN",sandbox_mode="workspace-write",shell_excluded_env_names:["ABCP_BROKER_SESSION_TOKEN"],token_ttl_ms:u64=900000,max_token_renewals:u64=5,token_request_budget:u64=32,session_request_budget:u64=128,session_request_bytes:u64=8388608,session_response_bytes:u64=268435456,connect_timeout_ms:u64=5000,request_timeout_ms:u64=300000,stream_idle_timeout_ms:u64=60000,session_timeout_ms:u64=5400000,transport_retries:u64=0,stream_reconnects:u64=0,loopback_only:bool=true,tool_network_must_be_denied:bool=true,parent_environ_must_be_denied:bool=true,model="gpt-5.6-sol",reasoning_effort="xhigh"` |
| `DogfoodPolicyFloorV1` / `dogfood-policy-floor-v1` | `kind,schema_version,parent_lineage_sha256,policy_manifest_sha256,required_failure_dimensions:[]FailureDimension(set),required_evidence_classes:[]EvidenceClass(set),required_final_review_profile_sha256,forbidden_operations:[]operation(set),max_child_budget_sha256:sha256<ResourceBudgetV1>,provider_isolation_profile_sha256:sha256<CodexProviderIsolationProfileV1>,tool_set_sha256:sha256<DogfoodToolSetV1>` |
| `DogfoodToolSetV1` / `dogfood-tool-set-v1` | `kind,schema_version,ralphex_sha256:sha256<ToolIdentityV1>,codex_sha256:sha256<ToolIdentityV1>` |
| `DogfoodChildActivationV1` / `dogfood-child-activation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,activation_mode="DOGFOOD_DELEGATED",parent_lineage_sha256,parent_integration_tip_sha256,parent_reservation_sha256:sha256<AssuranceResourceReservationV1>,parent_worker_reservation_sha256:sha256<WorkerCapacityReservationV1>,child_repository_identity,child_baseline_oid:gitOID,child_baseline_tree_oid:gitOID,child_plan_path:path,child_plan_sha256:blob256,policy_floor_sha256:sha256<DogfoodPolicyFloorV1>,child_budget_sha256:sha256<ResourceBudgetV1>,codex_provider_isolation_profile_sha256,tool_set_sha256,nesting_depth:u64=1,allowed_phases:["A_DESIGN"],allowed_operations:["design-planning","design-review"]` |
| `LedgerEventBindingV1` / `ledger-event-binding-v1` | `kind,schema_version,ledger_physical_identity_sha256:sha256<PathIdentityV1>,ledger_snapshot_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,schema_version_value:u64=1,event_id:hexid,timestamp:ledgerTimestamp,project_id?:text<=4096,plan_id?:text<=4096,run_id:id<=256,attempt_id?:text<=4096,task_id?:text<=4096,agent_session_id?:text<=4096,correlation_id?:text<=4096,event_type:id,state_from?:text<=64,state_to?:text<=64,actor:text<=4096,source:text<=4096,payload_sha256?:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,evidence_refs_sha256?:sha256<LedgerEvidenceRefListV1>,event_bytes_artifact_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,event_bytes_sha256:blob256`; optionality exactly follows `omitempty`; exact event bytes are one retained `ledger.Event` JSONL line without newline and every parsed field/payload/ref equals this binding. |
| `ProviderCredentialUseV1` / `provider-credential-use-v1` | `kind,schema_version,provider_id="github",capability_id:hexid,credential_kind,credential_role,principal_type,issued_at,expires_at?:timestamp,secret_byte_length:u64=1..4096,secret_commitment_sha256:blob256,authorization_scheme="Bearer",authorization_header_commitment_sha256:blob256,use_sha256:self`; secret commitment is `SHA256("ABCP-PROVIDER-CREDENTIAL-V1" || NUL || raw(capability_id) || NUL || exact credential bytes)` and header commitment is `SHA256("ABCP-PROVIDER-AUTHORIZATION-V1" || NUL || UTF8("Bearer ") || exact credential bytes)`. The nonce is the unguessable capability ID; raw credential/header bytes never enter a durable record. `MUTATION` permits USER_TOKEN or INSTALLATION_TOKEN; its non-copyable capability may issue only the typed identity/scope reads bound into its context and then exactly one admitted mutation, after which mutation/expiry zeroizes it. `APP_IDENTITY` permits only APP_JWT, may issue only the one typed `/app` identity read, can never cross a mutation transport boundary, and zeroizes after context construction or expiry. A later reconciliation uses a new typed observation credential/context while retaining the original mutation commitment in every chain record. |
| `ProviderReadRequestV1` / `provider-read-request-v1` | `kind,schema_version,provider_id="github",credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>,read_purpose,method="GET",base_url_origin:origin,route_template:routeTemplate,escaped_path:route,canonical_query:query,headers_sha256:sha256<StringMapV1>,response_bytes_limit:u64=1..4194304,parser,request_identity:hexid`; production origin/version/Accept headers equal `P-EFFECT-002`, Content-Type is absent, and route/purpose/parser is exactly: USER `/user`/empty/GITHUB_USER_V1; APP `/app`/empty/GITHUB_APP_V1; INSTALLATION `/installation`/empty/GITHUB_INSTALLATION_V1; user repository `/repos/{owner}/{repo}`/empty/GITHUB_REPOSITORY_V1; installation repositories `/installation/repositories`/`page=<n>&per_page=100`/GITHUB_INSTALLATION_REPOSITORIES_PAGE_V1; known-PR reconciliation `/repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/{number}`/empty/GITHUB_PULL_REQUEST_V1; response-free CREATE reconciliation `/repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls`/`base=<base>&direction=desc&head=<owner>%3A<head>&page=1&per_page=100&sort=created&state=all`/GITHUB_PULL_REQUEST_PAGE_V1; ref reconciliation `/repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/ref/heads/{percent-escaped-branch}`/empty/GITHUB_GIT_REF_V1; commit reconciliation `/repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/commits/{oid}`/empty/GITHUB_GIT_COMMIT_V1. Page `n` is decimal 1..100 and installation pages are gap-free; all placeholders are percent-escaped from the bound request, not caller text. Request identity is SHA-256 of `ABCP-PROVIDER-READ-V1` NUL credential-use digest NUL purpose NUL escaped path NUL query. |
| `ProviderReadResponseV1` / `provider-read-response-v1` | `kind,schema_version,request_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadRequestV1>,credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>,read_purpose,parser,http_status:u64=200,response_body_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,response_body_bytes:u64=1..4194304,response_headers_sha256:sha256<StringMapV1>,parsed_observation_sha256:sha256<GitHubUserObservationV1-or-GitHubAppObservationV1-or-GitHubInstallationObservationV1-or-GitHubRepositoryObservationV1-or-GitHubInstallationRepositoriesPageV1-or-GitHubPullRequestObservationV1-or-GitHubPullRequestPageObservationV1-or-GitHubGitRefObservationV1-or-GitHubGitCommitObservationV1>,observed_at,response_sha256:self`; parser/purpose selects the exact projection: USER requires root `/login:string,/id:positive-integer,/node_id:string`; APP root `/id,/node_id,/slug`; INSTALLATION root `/id,/app_id,/target_type:{User,Organization},/account/id,/account/node_id`; REPOSITORY and every page repository require `/owner/login,/name,/id,/node_id,/private:boolean,/permissions/pull|triage|push|maintain|admin:boolean`, with permission the strongest true value in `ADMIN>MAINTAIN>WRITE(push)>TRIAGE>READ(pull)`; installation page root requires `/total_count:0..10000,/repositories:array<=100`; PR requires `/number,/node_id,/state,/title,/body,/base/ref,/base/sha,/head/ref,/head/sha,/merged`; PR page is a root array of at most 100 such objects and uses the RFC8288 `Link rel="next"` response header; ref requires `/ref,/object/type="commit",/object/sha`; commit requires `/sha,/tree/sha`. The bounded UTF-8 JSON parser rejects invalid syntax, depth >32, more than 65,536 tokens, duplicate object keys at any depth, missing/wrong-typed selected pointers and trailing bytes; unselected members are skipped deterministically and cannot alter the projection. Response truncation and parser/purpose/credential mismatch fail. |
| `GitHubUserObservationV1` / `github-user-observation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,login:id,database_id:u64>=1,node_id:identity,actor_sha256:sha256<GitHubActingIdentityV1>`; actor is the retained `NewUserIdentity` value whose subject is exact UTF-8 `github-user:` + base-10 database ID + `:` + login + `:` + node ID. |
| `GitHubAppObservationV1` / `github-app-observation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,app_id:u64>=1,node_id:identity,slug:id,app_identity_credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>`; this proves app identity only and never repository scope. |
| `GitHubInstallationObservationV1` / `github-installation-observation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,installation_id:u64>=1,app_id:u64>=1,target_type:{User,Organization},target_database_id:u64>=1,target_node_id:identity,mutation_credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>` |
| `GitHubRepositoryObservationV1` / `github-repository-observation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,repository_owner:id,repository_name:id,repository_database_id:u64>=1,repository_node_id:identity,private:bool,permission,mutation_credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>`; permission must be WRITE/MAINTAIN/ADMIN. |
| `GitHubInstallationRepositoriesPageV1` / `github-installation-repositories-page-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,installation_id:u64>=1,page:u64=1..100,per_page:u64=100,total_count:u64=1..10000,repositories:[]GitHubScopedRepositoryV1(order:full_name,1..100),has_next:bool,page_sha256:self`; repositories are sorted by full name and each repeats owner/name/database/node/private/permission. Pages start at 1, are gap-free, `has_next` iff covered count is less than total, and at most 100 pages/10,000 repositories are admitted. |
| `GitHubPullRequestObservationV1` / `github-pull-request-observation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,pr_number:u64>=1,pr_node_id:identity,state:{open,closed},merged:bool,base_ref:ref,base_oid:gitOID,head_ref:ref,head_oid:gitOID,title:prTitle,body:prBody,document_sha256:blob256,observation_sha256:self`; refs are canonical `refs/heads/` values built from response branch names; JSON `body:null` deterministically maps to the empty `prBody`, while a string is preserved exactly; document digest uses the exact PR request formula. |
| `GitHubPullRequestPageObservationV1` / `github-pull-request-page-observation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,query_document_sha256:blob256,pull_requests:[]GitHubPullRequestObservationV1(order:pr_number,0..100),has_next:bool,page_sha256:self`; query document is exact canonical `{repository,base_ref,head_ref,title_sha256,body_sha256}` from the attempted CREATE. A decisive CREATE reconciliation requires `has_next=false` and exactly one entry equal to every selector/document field; zero proves NOT_APPLIED, more than one or a next page is UNKNOWN. |
| `GitHubGitRefObservationV1` / `github-git-ref-observation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,repository_identity:identity,ref:ref,object_type="commit",object_oid:gitOID,observation_sha256:self`; repository/ref equal the effect request. |
| `GitHubGitCommitObservationV1` / `github-git-commit-observation-v1` | `kind,schema_version,response_sha256:sha256<ProviderReadResponseV1>,repository_identity:identity,commit_oid:gitOID,tree_oid:gitOID,observation_sha256:self`; commit equals the immediately preceding ref observation object and the repository equals the effect request. |
| `ProviderAuthenticationContextV1` / `provider-authentication-context-v1` | `kind,schema_version,provider_id="github",principal_type,mutation_credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>,acting_actor_sha256:sha256<GitHubActingIdentityV1>,repository_identity:identity,repository_owner:id,repository_name:id,repository_database_id:u64>=1,repository_node_id:identity,user_observation_sha256?:sha256<GitHubUserObservationV1>,user_repository_observation_sha256?:sha256<GitHubRepositoryObservationV1>,app_identity_credential_use_sha256?:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>,app_observation_sha256?:sha256<GitHubAppObservationV1>,installation_observation_sha256?:sha256<GitHubInstallationObservationV1>,installation_repository_page_sha256s:[]sha256<GitHubInstallationRepositoriesPageV1>(order,0..100),observed_at,valid_through,context_sha256:self`; `P-EFFECT-001` requires USER to use one USER_TOKEN as the same mutation credential in `/user`, repository read and later mutation, with only the two user arms and acting actor exactly the user observation actor. APP requires one APP_JWT only for `/app`, while the same INSTALLATION_TOKEN mutation credential appears in `/installation`, every `/installation/repositories` page and later mutation; app ID equals installation app ID, installation ID is exact, and the complete gap-free pages include exactly the authorized repository with matching owner/name/database/node identity and WRITE/MAINTAIN/ADMIN. Its acting actor is retained `NewAppInstallationIdentity` with subject exact UTF-8 `github-app:` + base-10 app ID + `:` + slug and installation ID from the typed installation. App identity alone is never scope. Repository identity is the retained configured repository binding and the four provider fields must equal its GitHub repository fields. All observations precede `valid_through`; it is at most 300 seconds after the last read and, when the mutation credential has `expires_at`, no later than that expiry. |
| `AuthenticatedProviderPrincipalV1` / `authenticated-provider-principal-v1` | `kind,schema_version,provider_id="github",principal_type,principal_subject:identity,application_id?:identity,installation_id?:identity,acting_actor:identity,repository_scope:identity,mutation_credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>,authentication_context_sha256:sha256<ProviderAuthenticationContextV1>,observed_at,valid_through`; fields are a projection of the typed context, not assertions. USER uses the exact retained actor subject and omits app IDs. APP principal subject is `github-app-installation:` plus decimal installation ID, application/installation IDs are their base-10 strings, and acting actor is the retained actor subject. Repository scope is exact `github-repository:` + decimal database ID + `:` + node ID + `:` + owner + `/` + name. App/application/installation and user arms follow `P-EFFECT-001`. No reusable credential bytes are retained. |
| `PRPublicationAuthorityV1` / `pr-publication-authority-v1` | `kind,schema_version,repository:GitHubRepositoryV1,repository_identity:identity,repository_node_id:identity,base_branch:branch,head_branch:branch,head_sha:gitOID,expected_base_tip_sha:gitOID,expected_head_tree_sha:gitOID,pull_request?:GitHubPullRequestIdentityV1,title_sha256:blob256,body_sha256:blob256,document_sha256:blob256,limits_sha256:sha256<GitHubLifecycleLimitsV1>,allowed_merge_method:merge_method,actor:GitHubActingIdentityV1,integration_accepted_event_sha256:sha256<LedgerEventBindingV1>,integration_tip_sha256:sha256<PhaseCheckpointV2>,combined_acceptance_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,pinned_git_sha256:sha256<ToolIdentityV1>,authority_sha256:self`; head/base/tree and complete PR title/body/document/limit digests are derived directly from the exact INTEGRATION_ACCEPTED decision, controller PR document and pinned local Git. The document digest is over exact canonical `{"title":title,"body":body}` bytes. It contains no READY binding, READY evidence, merge policy or retained `ExpectedMergeContentV1`. |
| `PRLifecycleUpsertPayloadV1` / `pr-lifecycle-upsert-payload-v1` | `kind,schema_version,authority_sha256:sha256<PRPublicationAuthorityV1>,title:prTitle,body:prBody,limits_sha256:sha256<GitHubLifecycleLimitsV1>,payload_sha256:self`; title/body/document and limits must reproduce the digests already sealed by the authority. This is the additive pre-READY upsert payload, not the inherited complete merge payload. |
| `PRLifecycleWriteAttemptV1` / `pr-lifecycle-write-attempt-v1` | `kind,schema_version,repository:GitHubRepositoryV1,actor:GitHubActingIdentityV1,operation_kind="pr_upsert",write_id:id,authority_sha256:sha256<PRPublicationAuthorityV1>,canonical_payload_sha256:sha256<PRLifecycleUpsertPayloadV1>,limits_sha256:sha256<GitHubLifecycleLimitsV1>,attempt_sha256:self`; it contains no READY/policy digest. |
| `GitHubLifecycleAuthorityV1` / inherited `githublifecycle.Authority` | Exact input-head complete merge `Authority.CanonicalJSON` order/validators: `repository:GitHubRepositoryV1,base_branch:branch,head_branch:branch,head_sha:gitOID,expected_base_tip_sha:gitOID,pull_request:GitHubPullRequestIdentityV1,allowed_merge_method:merge_method,actor:GitHubActingIdentityV1,expected_merge_content:ExpectedMergeContentV1,ready_authority_binding:ReadyAuthorityBindingV1,ready_authority_binding_sha256:sha256<ReadyAuthorityBindingV1>,merge_policy:MergePolicyV1,merge_policy_sha256:sha256<MergePolicyV1>`. It is merge-only in V4; all named nested retained types keep exact input-head bytes/constructors and remain catalog entries. |
| `GitHubMergeAuthorityAdapterV1` / `github-merge-authority-adapter-v1` | `kind,schema_version,pr_publication_authority_sha256:sha256<PRPublicationAuthorityV1>,ready_evidence_sha256:sha256<ReadyForMergeEvidenceV1>,ready_authority_binding_sha256:sha256<ReadyAuthorityBindingV1>,merge_policy_sha256:sha256<MergePolicyV1>,expected_merge_content_sha256:sha256<ExpectedMergeContentV1>,pull_request:GitHubPullRequestIdentityV1,github_lifecycle_authority_sha256:sha256<GitHubLifecycleAuthorityV1>,adapter_sha256:self`; after exact PR APPLIED/READY only, it copies repository/base/head/tree/actor/method from pre-READY authority, inserts returned PR identity, derives retained READY binding/expected content/policy, and re-runs the existing complete `NewAuthority` constructor byte-for-byte. |
| `ProviderRequestV1` / `provider-request-v1` | `kind,schema_version,effect_kind,provider_id:id,authenticated_principal_sha256:sha256<AuthenticatedProviderPrincipalV1>,authentication_context_sha256:sha256<ProviderAuthenticationContextV1>,mutation_credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>,method,base_url_origin:origin,route_template:routeTemplate,escaped_path:route,canonical_query:query,headers_sha256:sha256<StringMapV1>,authorization_header_commitment_sha256:blob256,body_artifact_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,body_sha256:blob256,body_bytes:u64<=1048576,response_bytes_limit:u64<=33554432,request_identity:id`; `P-EFFECT-002` requires body artifact bytes/digest/size equal transport bytes, production origin, empty query and canonical non-secret headers exactly `Accept=application/vnd.github+json`, `Content-Type=application/json`, `X-GitHub-Api-Version=2026-03-10`. Authorization bytes are absent but their commitment equals the mutation credential record. Immediately before transport, the non-durable `BoundProviderCredentialCapabilityV1.Take()` recomputes both commitments from its exact bytes, checks capability/context/request/admission equality and injects that one Authorization value; it is then consumed. PR response limit is 4,194,304 bytes; merge uses retained EP-005 limit. Test loopback can satisfy unit cells only. |
| `PREffectRequestV1` / `pr-effect-request-v1` | `kind,schema_version,provider_request_sha256:sha256<ProviderRequestV1>,authenticated_principal_sha256:sha256<AuthenticatedProviderPrincipalV1>,authentication_context_sha256:sha256<ProviderAuthenticationContextV1>,mutation_credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>,pr_publication_authority_sha256:sha256<PRPublicationAuthorityV1>,upsert_payload_sha256:sha256<PRLifecycleUpsertPayloadV1>,write_attempt_sha256:sha256<PRLifecycleWriteAttemptV1>,pr_resource_key:hexid,pr_revision_ordinal:u64>=1,repository_identity:identity,repository_owner:id,repository_name:id,repository_node_id:identity,base_branch:branch,base_ref:ref,base_oid:gitOID,head_branch:branch,head_ref:ref,head_oid:gitOID,mutation_mode,pr_number?:u64>=1,pr_node_id?:identity,title:prTitle,body:prBody,title_sha256:blob256,body_sha256:blob256,document_sha256:blob256,request_identity:id`; `P-PR-001` requires pre-READY authority/auth-context/credential/repository/branches/OIDs/method/actor and optional PR equality, refs exactly `refs/heads/<branch>`, title/body digests over exact UTF-8, and document digest over exact canonical `{"title":title,"body":body}`. UPDATE carries both PR fields; CREATE neither. Resource key and request identity retain the exact existing formulas; additive upsert payload/attempt rebuild with pre-READY authority and contain no READY bytes. `P-PR-002` derives existing CREATE/UPDATE method/routes/bodies exactly as before; raw request credential/context/principal/repository/route/body/identity must equal this derivation and body limit is 16,384. |
| `TargetSubmissionV1` / inherited `target-submission-v1` | Exact existing canonical order from `internal/githublifecycle` at input head: `schema="target-submission-v1",sealed_authorization_sha256:sha256<SealedMergeAuthorizationV1>,authorization_seal_sha256:sha256<AuthorizationSealV1>,commitment_sha256:sha256<TargetRefCommitmentV1>,write_id:id,client_mutation_id:id,invocation_id:id,method="POST",path="/graphql",request_body:RAW_JSON[1..16384 bytes],request_body_sha256:blob256,request_body_bytes:u64=1..16384,limits_sha256:sha256<GitHubLifecycleLimitsV1>`; `P-MERGE-000` requires client mutation ID=write ID, body digest/size equality, and body exact canonical `UpdateRefs` document/variables deterministically rebuilt from the sealed authorization. The three referenced existing record types retain their exact input-head validators and are descriptor catalog entries, not opaque strings. |

MUTATION_RESPONSE omits the two observation credential/context fields and has exact-empty read arrays; it is parsed from the response to the request sent with that same consumed mutation capability. READ_ONLY_RECONCILIATION requires both observation fields and equal nonempty parallel arrays: a fresh non-durable read credential, independently complete typed auth/scope context for the same principal/repository, and each request/response credential equal to that observation credential. UPDATE uses exactly one known-PR read; response-free CREATE uses exactly one bounded selector page and its decisive/UNKNOWN rules above. It never replaces the original mutation triple. APPLIED is authenticated and repeats exact repository/refs/OIDs/title/body/document plus returned PR identity (equal requested UPDATE); NOT_APPLIED is typed authenticated absence/non-application for exact CREATE selector or UPDATE; UNKNOWN grants no inference. |

MUTATION_RESPONSE has exact-empty read arrays. READ_ONLY_RECONCILIATION has exactly two parallel reads in order: bound base ref, then that returned commit; APPLIED requires ref OID differ from `base_before_oid`, commit/tree equality and every request semantic, NOT_APPLIED requires ref still exactly `base_before_oid`, and any changed ref without the exact expected governed result is UNKNOWN. |

PR refs are exactly `(ready evidence artifact digest,"ready-for-merge-evidence-v1")` then `(PR observation artifact digest,"pr-provider-observation-v1")`; merge refs are exactly `(TargetSubmission artifact digest,"target-submission-v1")` then `(merge observation artifact digest,"merge-provider-observation-v1")`. Construct one retained `ledger.Event` without `NewEvent`, assigning fields in struct order and the typed payload map/ref list; Go 1.23 `encoding/json.Marshal` with HTML escaping enabled produces the exact no-newline bytes. | `EffectBarrierResolutionV1` / `effect-barrier-resolution-v1` | `kind,schema_version,effect_kind,intent_sha256:sha256<PREffectIntentV1-or-MergeEffectIntentV1>,claim_sha256?:sha256<PREffectClaimV1-or-MergeEffectClaimV1>,settlement_sha256?:sha256<PREffectSettlementV1-or-MergeEffectSettlementV1>,transition_barrier_sha256:sha256<EffectTransitionBarrierV1>,ledger_barrier_sha256:sha256<EffectLedgerBarrierV4>,removal_proof_sha256:sha256<EffectBarrierRemovalProofV1>,provider_request_sha256:sha256<PREffectRequestV1-or-MergeEffectRequestV1>,resolution_reason,resolved_at_pg_revision:u64,resolution_outcome="RESOLVED"`; `P-BARRIER-007` requires the ledger boundary to validate the proof/embedded commitment before exact barrier removal. UNCLAIMED omits claim/settlement; settled reasons require both and exact RESOLVE. Publication occurs only after fresh read proves that exact barrier absent. |
| `EffectReauthorizationV1` / `effect-reauthorization-v1` | `kind,schema_version,effect_kind,prior_intent_sha256:sha256<PREffectIntentV1-or-MergeEffectIntentV1>,prior_settlement_sha256:sha256<PREffectSettlementV1-or-MergeEffectSettlementV1>,prior_barrier_resolution_sha256:sha256<EffectBarrierResolutionV1>,settled_disposition="NOT_APPLIED",prior_effect_ordinal:u64,new_effect_ordinal:u64,current_ledger_event_sha256:sha256<LedgerEventBindingV1>,current_stage_tip_sha256:sha256<PhaseCheckpointV2>,prior_provider_request_sha256:sha256<PREffectRequestV1-or-MergeEffectRequestV1>,new_provider_request_sha256:sha256<PREffectRequestV1-or-MergeEffectRequestV1>,authorized_at_revision:u64`; prior/new request kind equals `effect_kind`, prior request equals the settled attempt, new request equals the next intent, its request identity binds the new ordinal, the two digests differ, prior barrier resolution must be exact, `new_effect_ordinal=prior_effect_ordinal+1`, and current event/tip must independently remain admissible. |
| `EffectAttemptIndexV1` / `effect-attempt-index-v1` | `kind,schema_version,effect_kind,effect_ordinal:u64>=1,predecessor_index_sha256?:sha256<EffectAttemptIndexV1>,intent_sha256:sha256<PREffectIntentV1-or-MergeEffectIntentV1>,claim_sha256?:sha256<PREffectClaimV1-or-MergeEffectClaimV1>,call_admission_sha256?:sha256<EffectCallAdmissionV1>,provider_request_sha256:sha256<PREffectRequestV1-or-MergeEffectRequestV1>,authentication_context_sha256:sha256<ProviderAuthenticationContextV1>,mutation_credential_use_sha256:sha256<ProviderCredentialUseV1>,result_sha256?:sha256<PREffectResultV1-or-MergeEffectResultV1>,reconciliation_anchor_sha256?:sha256<EffectReconciliationAnchorV1>,reconciliation_tip_sha256?:sha256<PREffectReconciliationV1-or-MergeEffectReconciliationV1>,settlement_sha256?:sha256<PREffectSettlementV1-or-MergeEffectSettlementV1>,barrier_resolution_sha256?:sha256<EffectBarrierResolutionV1>,index_revision:u64>=1,index_sha256:self`; union arms select by kind and all repeated chain/credential digests agree. Ordinal 1 omits predecessor; ordinal n>1 requires predecessor to be the same-kind terminal resolved ordinal n-1 index named by its reauthorization, so replacing the controller's current pointer retains a state-rooted chain to every earlier result/anchor/reconciliation/settlement. Before admission all outcome slots are empty. Exactly one of result/anchor may become first predecessor; anchor requires result absent under the same locked expected revision. Reconciliation requires one first predecessor; settlement names result/reconciliation/pre-call closure as appropriate; resolution requires settlement. Slots never clear or change to conflicting bytes. |
| `PostMergeFailureV1` / `post-merge-failure-v1` | `kind,schema_version,accepted_a_lineage_sha256,merge_result_oid:gitOID,merge_result_tree_oid:gitOID,ledger_terminal_event_sha256:sha256<LedgerEventBindingV1>,post_merge_evidence_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,reason_code,created_sequence:u64>=1`; coordination state is `POST_MERGE_FAILED`, while prior MERGED event bytes remain immutable. |
| `ControllerStateV2` / `governance-controller-state-v2` | `kind,schema_version,authority_domain:authorityDomain,controller_identity:dbIdentity,repository_identity,revision:u64,predecessor_state_v1_artifact_sha256,predecessor_state_v1_revision:u64,predecessor_cutover_sha256,activation_v2_sha256,active_lineage_sha256?:sha256<AcceptedALineageV1>,stage_generation:u64,coordination_state,active_stage_tip_sha256?:sha256<PhaseCheckpointV2>,invalidated_stage_tip_sha256?:sha256<PhaseCheckpointV2>,ledger_event_tip_sha256?:sha256<LedgerEventBindingV1>,a_required:bool,correction_reentries_used:u64<=2,active_resource_reservations:[]sha256<AssuranceResourceReservationV1>(set),selected_stage_evidence_sha256?:sha256<StageEvidenceSelectionV1>,pr_effect_attempt_index_sha256?:sha256<EffectAttemptIndexV1>,merge_effect_attempt_index_sha256?:sha256<EffectAttemptIndexV1>,effect_attempt_state,cumulative_resource_counters:ResourceCountersV2`; each current effect chain is reachable only through its typed index. Result/observation artifact create-or-verify and state CAS to the new index occur in one SERIALIZABLE transaction; anchor does the same competing expected-revision CAS after locked result absence. Fresh-process recovery loads state→index→typed blobs and can prove a slot absent at the exact revision before any next action. |

`P-RESOURCE-003` additionally fixes nesting representation: root workflow/worker rows have depth 0, omit both parents and use `ROOT_CHARGE`; the sole J-05 child rows have depth 1, require the exact shown parent workflow/worker digests and frozen parent envelope revision, and use `INCLUSIVE_CHILD_SUBALLOCATION`; every other depth or parent/accounting combination rejects.

For either provider observation, `raw_response_artifact_sha256` is the exact mutation response body artifact for `MUTATION_RESPONSE`. For `READ_ONLY_RECONCILIATION` it is the artifact containing canonical no-whitespace JSON `{"provider_read_response_sha256s":[<the response digests in observation order>]}`; this bundle and every referenced response/body artifact commit with the observation/result/index transaction. Thus a multi-read reconciliation has one exact retained byte root without conflating it with the original mutation response.

In the V4 event-ID formula, every variable component is UTF-8: effect kind and destination are their exact enum/state literals, while intent/outcome are their exact 64-byte lower-case hexadecimal digest strings. Each adjacent component is separated by exactly one `0x00`; no decoded digest bytes, length prefixes or final NUL are used. Event JSON is exactly the single `json.Marshal` result with no trailing LF, BOM or surrounding JSONL whitespace; the ledger adds its existing single LF only as storage framing and excludes that LF from both event digests.

Nested records are exact: `PolicyFileV1={path:path,sha256:blob256}`; `OperationContextV1={kind:operation,owned_scope:[]path(set,0..256),blocking_criteria:[]text(set,0..256)}`; `SourceV1={path:path,sha256:blob256}`; `OutcomeV1={task:id,summary:text,commit_sha:gitOID}`; `EvidenceBindingV1={ref:text<=2048,sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>}`; `LifecycleBindingV1` is the exact current V1 field order and bounds; `ExecutionBoundsV1` is the exact current V1 field order, canonical Go durations and positive current validators.

`CheckpointGrantParentV2` is fully materialized—not an implicit Go projection—with this exact order and types: `{kind:checkpoint_kind,repository:identity,capsule_file_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,capsule_sha256:sha256<ContextCapsuleV4>,candidate_sha:gitOID,operation,verdict:text<=128,evidence:[]EvidenceBindingV1(set:ref,0..256),controller_policy_identity:identity,sequence:u64,predecessor_checkpoint_sha256?:sha256<PhaseCheckpointV2>,controller_event_identity:identity,semantic_registry_sha256:sha256<SemanticAuthorityRegistryV2>,review_scope_tip_sha256?:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,acceptance_result_sha256?:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,review_critical:u64,review_major:u64,reviewed_blocking_scope_ids:[]id(set,0..256),lifecycle?:LifecycleBindingV1,schema_version="phase-checkpoint-v2",repository_identity:identity,accepted_a_lineage_sha256?:sha256<AcceptedALineageV1>,stage:phase,candidate_tree_oid:gitOID,ledger_event_sha256?:sha256<LedgerEventBindingV1>,policy_manifest_sha256:sha256<AssurancePolicyManifestV1>,assurance_model_sha256:sha256<AssuranceModelV1>,proof_obligation_set_sha256:sha256<ProofObligationSetV1>,evidence_matrix_sha256:sha256<EvidenceMatrixV1>,resource_profile_set_sha256:sha256<ResourceProfileSetV1>,canonical_vector_catalog_sha256:sha256<CanonicalVectorCatalogV1>,work_classification_sha256:sha256<WorkClassificationV1>,final_review_profile_sha256:sha256<FinalReviewProfileV1>,evidence_selection_sha256?:sha256<StageEvidenceSelectionV1>}`. It is byte-for-byte the `PhaseCheckpointV2` canonical preimage with only `next_stage_grant_sha256` and terminal `checkpoint_sha256` omitted; `P-PARENT-001` validates each copied value against the full checkpoint.

The other nested descriptors are: `ScenarioV1={id:id,dimension:FailureDimension,scenario:text,invariant_ids:[]id(set,1..256),proof_ids:[]id(set,1..256)}`; `InvariantV1={id:id,claim:text}`; `ProofV1={id:id,claim:text,invariant_ids:[]id(set,1..256)}`; `ValidatorV1={id:id,stage:evidence_stage,command:command,purpose:text,materialized_resource_profile_id:id,materialized_resource_profile_sha256:sha256<MaterializedResourceProfileV1>,secret_capabilities:[]secret_capability(set,1..4),requires_postgres:bool,requires_container:bool}`; `CellV1={id:id,proof_id:id,evidence_class:EvidenceClass,stage:evidence_stage,subject_rule:text,validator_id:id}`; `JourneyV1={id:id,validator_id:id,title:text,subject_rule:text,required_boundary_ids:[]id(set,1..256)}`; `ComponentScopeV1={id:id,allowed_paths:[]path(set,1..256)}`; `SemanticRuleV2={rule_id:id,proof_id:id,correction_relation,owner_component:id,allowed_scope_ids:[]id(set,1..256)}`; `SelectedCellV1={cell_id:id,evidence_sha256:sha256<AssuranceEvidenceCellV1>,reservation_sha256:sha256<AssuranceResourceReservationV1>}`.

`PredecessorStoreBindingV1={binding_id:id,binding_kind,run_id?:id,host_identity:dbIdentity,canonical_path_sha256?:sha256<PathIdentityV1>,physical_identity_sha256?:sha256<PathIdentityV1>,workflow_backend_identity_sha256?:sha256<WorkflowBackendIdentityV1>,initial_scan_artifact_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,barrier_state_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,registered_epoch:u64}`; WORKFLOW_STATE requires only backend identity; file-backed LEDGER/PR/MERGE bindings require both absolute path identities and omit backend identity. `WorkflowBackendIdentityV1={authority_domain:authorityDomain,controller_identity:dbIdentity,implementation_id:id,composition_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>}`.

`EffectBarrierCommitmentV1={effect_kind,run_id:id<=256,effect_ordinal:u64>=1,source_ledger_event_sha256:sha256<LedgerEventBindingV1>,source_state:text<=64,intent_sha256:sha256<PREffectIntentV1-or-MergeEffectIntentV1>,provider_request_sha256:sha256<PREffectRequestV1-or-MergeEffectRequestV1>,applied_state_to:text<=64,not_applied_action="NO_LEDGER_TRANSITION",unknown_action="RETAIN_BARRIER"}`. `P-BARRIER-008` fixes PR source/target to `INTEGRATION_ACCEPTED/READY_FOR_MERGE`, merge to `READY_FOR_MERGE/MERGED`, selects all union arms by effect kind, and requires the event/intention/request/run/ordinal chain to be exact. Legacy `TransitionBarrierV1` remains historical V3 bytes only and is explicitly not operational authority for a V4 effect.

`ArtifactInventoryEntryV2={occurrence_ordinal:u64,occurrence_sha256:sha256<ArtifactOccurrenceV1>,artifact_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,artifact_kind,outcome,byte_size:u64}`; `CredentialUseV1={secret_id:id,secret_capability,acquisition_identity_sha256:sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>,value_commitment_sha256:blob256,byte_length:u64}`; `ContainerImageIdentityV1={image_reference:text<=2048,image_digest:text<=256,platform:text<=64,compressed_bytes_limit:u64,unpacked_bytes_limit:u64}`; `PathIdentityV1={canonical_absolute_path:absPath,device:u64,inode:u64,mode:u64}`; `RepositoryPathV1={repository_relative_path:path}`. `GitHubScopedRepositoryV1={full_name:text<=257,owner:id,name:id,database_id:u64>=1,node_id:identity,private:bool,permission:repository_permission}` and `full_name=owner+"/"+name`. `LedgerEvidenceRefV4={uri:artifactURI,sha256:blob256,kind:id}`, where `artifactURI` is the exact 87-byte ASCII form `abcp-artifact://sha256/` plus 64 lower-case hex. `StringListV1={values:[]text(order,0..256)}`; `StringMapV1={entries:[]KeyValueV1(set:key,0..256)}`; `KeyValueV1={key:text<=4096,value:text<=4096}`; `EnvironmentPolicyV1={allow:[]id(set,0..256),deny:[]id(set,0..256),fixed:[]KeyValueV1(set:key,0..256),inherit_none:bool}`; `ToolchainIdentityV1={go_version:text<=128,go_executable_sha256:blob256,module_graph_sha256:blob256,os:id,arch:id}`; `MerkleRootV1={algorithm="SHA256_DOMAIN_SEPARATED_V1",leaf_count:u64,root_sha256:blob256}`.

Retained GitHub aliases are exact cataloged nested types: `GitHubRepositoryV1={owner:id,name:id}` using input-head `repoWire`; `GitHubPullRequestIdentityV1={number:u64>=1,node_id:identity}` using `prIdentityWire`; and `GitHubActingIdentityV1={kind:{user,app_installation},subject:text<=256,installation_id?:u64>=1}` using `actorWire`, where USER omits installation and APP requires it. Their digest is SHA-256 of that exact nested canonical object when a `sha256<...>` reference is used. `GitHubLifecycleLimitsV1` is the exact input-head `githublifecycle.Limits.CanonicalJSON` record and digest. `ExpectedMergeContentV1`, `ReadyAuthorityBindingV1`, `MergePolicyV1`, `SealedMergeAuthorizationV1`, `AuthorizationSealV1` and `TargetRefCommitmentV1` likewise mean their exact input-head canonical record/validator/digest; they are extracted as retained catalog entries and are never opaque JSON or newly chosen schemas.

The inventory Merkle preimage is also exact. Decode each 64-hex digest to 32 raw bytes and encode integers unsigned big-endian 8 bytes. A leaf is `SHA256("ABCP-INVENTORY-LEAF-V1" || NUL || ordinal || occurrence_digest || artifact_digest || UTF8(artifact_kind) || NUL || UTF8(outcome) || NUL || byte_size)`. An internal node is `SHA256("ABCP-INVENTORY-NODE-V1" || NUL || left || right)`; duplicate the final node at each odd-width level. Leaves are in increasing contiguous occurrence ordinal. The zero-leaf root is `SHA256("ABCP-INVENTORY-EMPTY-V1" || NUL)=c7e346bce459a39793ea809b51116df91c2316e7a48fcf53e8429864658ed463`. `MerkleRootV1.leaf_count/root_sha256`, the page entries and manifest count must agree exactly.

Digest references are never untyped strings. Inline targets above are authoritative. The exhaustive bare-digest registry is: every `accepted_a_lineage_sha256→AcceptedALineageV1`; `failed_stage_sha256→FailedStageV1`; `failed_c_capsule_sha256→ContextCapsuleV4`; `prior_c_tip_sha256/predecessor_stage_tip_sha256/current_stage_tip_sha256/integration_tip_sha256/final_review_tip_sha256/pr_published_tip_sha256/merge_authorized_tip_sha256→PhaseCheckpointV2`; `claim_token_sha256→blob256` over exactly 32 raw OS-CSPRNG bytes; `original_b_scope_sha256→PathSetV1`; `lineage_budget_sha256/remaining_resource_budget_sha256/max_child_budget_sha256/child_budget_sha256→ResourceBudgetV1`; `worker_capacity_sha256→WorkerCapacityV1`; `activation_v2_sha256→GovernanceActivationV2`; `predecessor_cutover_sha256→PredecessorCutoverV1`; all source/current/terminal/integration/ready ledger-event-named fields `→LedgerEventBindingV1`; `event_preimage_sha256→EffectLedgerEventPreimageV1`; `expected_event_bytes_sha256→blob256`; PR request fields `→PREffectRequestV1`; merge request fields `→MergeEffectRequestV1`; generic `provider_request_sha256` selects exactly one of those by effect kind; `authentication_context_sha256/observation_authentication_context_sha256→ProviderAuthenticationContextV1`; `mutation_credential_use_sha256/app_identity_credential_use_sha256/credential_use_sha256/observation_credential_use_sha256→ProviderCredentialUseV1`; `pr_publication_authority_sha256→PRPublicationAuthorityV1`; `merge_authority_adapter_sha256→GitHubMergeAuthorityAdapterV1`; `runtime_owner_sha256→RuntimeOwnerIdentityV1`; `observer_key_sha256→WorkerObservationKeyV1`; each typed absence digest targets its named process/cgroup/container observation; `allocation_profile_sha256/direct_profile_sha256/allocation_materialized_resource_profile_sha256/direct_materialized_resource_profile_sha256/materialized_resource_profile_sha256→MaterializedResourceProfileV1`; `result_sha256`, `reconciliation_anchor_sha256`, `reconciliation_tip_sha256`, `settlement_sha256` and `barrier_resolution_sha256` select the exact effect/kind arm declared by `EffectAttemptIndexV1`; `environment_policy_sha256→EnvironmentPolicyV1`; `toolchain_sha256→ToolchainIdentityV1`; `inventory_sha256→RetainedArtifactInventoryV2`; `secret_scan_sha256→SecretScanEvidenceV2`; `stage_subject_sha256/subject_sha256→StageSubjectV1` where the row so names a stage subject; `candidate_diff_sha256→CandidateDiffArtifactV1`; `secret_set_commitment_sha256→StageSecretSetV1`; settlement and provider-observation fields select the effect-specific named type; and every suffix `*_artifact_sha256→ArtifactBlobV1`. Repeated bare assurance digests inherit the exact target declared in `AcceptedALineageV1`. `effect_kind` selects the corresponding PR or merge type for every intent/claim/result/reconciliation/settlement union and cross-kind references fail. Terminal self-digests target their containing record with that terminal field absent. `sha256<ArtifactBlobV1>` equals that blob row's `artifact_sha256`, the digest of exact bytes rather than metadata. A bare digest absent from this registry, an inline annotation or an inherited-record descriptor is a model error, not a string.

### Exhaustive canonical vector catalog

`CanonicalVectorCatalogV1` is itself `kind,schema_version,descriptor_version="CANONICAL-VECTOR-CATALOG-V3",entries:[]CanonicalVectorEntryV1(set:schema_id,1..256),catalog_sha256:self`; an entry is `schema_id:id,record_name:id,fields:[]WireFieldDescriptorV1(order,1..256),predicates:[]PredicateDescriptorV1(set:predicate_id,0..256),positive_recipe="MINIMAL-VALID-V3",rejections:[]CanonicalRejectionVectorV1(set:vector_id,1..4096)`, a predicate is `predicate_id:id,schema_id:id,field_paths:[]text(set,1..64),operator:{ALL_EQUAL,EXACT_LITERAL,IF_AND_ONLY_IF,IMPLIES,EXACTLY_ONE,SUBSET,ORDINAL_SUCCESSOR,STATE_TRANSITION,TYPED_REFERENCE,DIGEST_PREIMAGE,DERIVATION,AGGREGATE_LEQ},arguments:[]text(order,0..64),required_error="PREDICATE_INVALID"`, and a rejection is `vector_id:text<=512,mutation:text<=128,required_error`. A top-level record's `schema_id` is its exact wire `schema_version`; a nested record's is `nested:` followed by its exact record name. The recipe and mutations generate bytes rather than embedding them, so the finite catalog includes entries for the catalog/entry/field/predicate/rejection descriptors without recursive vector bytes. The catalog must contain exactly one entry for every top-level, inherited and nested record named in this section—no allowlist maintained elsewhere. `AG-VAL-B-021` extracts those names and descriptors from the immutable A model and rejects missing/extra entries.

Predicate enumeration is total and stable. Literal, optional-arm, equality, typed-reference, self-digest, derivation and state clauses written inline produce IDs `P-<schema-id>-<OPERATOR>-<first-field-path>`; collision suffix is the one-based field-order ordinal. A clause already carrying `P-*` uses that exact ID. Each retained unlabelled cross-field clause in a row is assigned `P-<schema-id>-ROW-<NNN>`, where NNN is its one-based left-to-right semicolon clause ordinal after code-span-aware splitting. Referenced field paths are the field tokens named in that clause, sorted by descriptor ordinal; if none is named, the sole path is `$` for the complete record. Operator selection is the first matching token in this frozen precedence: `sum→AGGREGATE_LEQ`, `transition→STATE_TRANSITION`, `successor/ordinal→ORDINAL_SUCCESSOR`, `subset/narrow→SUBSET`, `exactly one→EXACTLY_ONE`, `iff/if and only if→IF_AND_ONLY_IF`, `requires/only/when→IMPLIES`, `digest/hash→DIGEST_PREIMAGE`, `reference/target/type→TYPED_REFERENCE`, `equal/match/repeat→ALL_EQUAL`, otherwise `DERIVATION`; arguments are the exact normalized clause bytes. Thus zero or multiple keyword matches introduce no B choice. The generated list—not free prose—is the authoritative predicate enumeration. The named correction predicates are exactly `P-SUBJECT-001..002`, `P-DIFF-001..005`, `P-PUBLISHER-001..007`, `P-RESOURCE-001..003`, `P-PARENT-001`, `P-EFFECT-001..002`, `P-PR-001..003`, `P-MERGE-000..003`, `P-EVENT-001`, `P-BARRIER-001..008`, `P-RECONCILE-001..002`, and `P-READY-001`; duplicate, missing, uncovered or non-executable IDs fail A-model validation.

Positive bytes are deterministic, not hand-selected by B: the generator uses extracted field order/type/primitive bounds; exact constants; first enum value; integer lower bound; `"a"` for text/id; minimal valid branch/ref/origin/route/path/absPath/authorityDomain; `"a"*40` for `gitOID`; `"a"*64` for hex/digests/dbIdentity; exact minimum cardinality; sorted sets; optional fields omitted; then evaluates predicates in ID order and recomputes self digests. Literal values override only the positive value, not primitive bounds. The raw `ArtifactBlobV1` vector remains byte `0x61` with real digest/size. For every descriptor constraint the catalog contains the prior removal/null/type/bound/enum/digest/order/canonical rejections, every literal has `CONSTANT_CHANGED`, and every predicate has exactly one rejection. `required_error` remains `{NON_CANONICAL,UNKNOWN_FIELD,DUPLICATE_FIELD,MISSING_FIELD,TYPE_INVALID,BOUND_INVALID,ENUM_INVALID,DIGEST_INVALID,REFERENCE_TYPE_INVALID,ORDER_INVALID,PREDICATE_INVALID}`. Missing type/bound/target/literal base type/row field/predicate or an unstoreable DDL identity fails extraction before a positive vector exists.

Vector naming is unchanged: `<schema_id>/<RFC6901-target-or-$>/<mutation-code>`, with closed codes `{REMOVE,NULL,WRONG_TYPE,BELOW_MIN,ABOVE_MAX,OVERSIZE,ENUM_INVALID,UPPERCASE,SHORT,BAD_CHAR,UNSORTED,DUPLICATE_MEMBER,CONSTANT_CHANGED,UNKNOWN_FIRST,UNKNOWN_LAST,DUPLICATE_FIELD,INVALID_UTF8,TRAILING_JSON,SELF_DIGEST_MISMATCH,PREDICATE_<frozen-predicate-id>}`. Ordinary codes keep their single mutation. `CONSTANT_CHANGED` deliberately ignores the literal predicate while remaining valid for the primitive descriptor: a literal whose primitive is a closed enum becomes the lexicographically first different member of that enum; otherwise scan a string/id/path-like literal from the last byte leftward for the first byte whose replacement preserves primitive syntax/length and replace digit `0..8` with its decimal successor, digit `9` with `8`, or a non-digit with the first differing byte in ordered alphabet `a,b,0,1,-,_`. If no such replacement exists, extraction fails. Integer literals use `v+1` if within primitive maximum else `v-1`; boolean literals flip; literal-array elements apply the enum or string rule to the lowest element ordinal. Thus `policy_version="context-capsule-v4"` deterministically becomes primitive-valid `context-capsule-v5` and yields `PREDICATE_INVALID`.

`PREDICATE_` uses this closed operator mutation table, never “next individually valid value”: `EXACT_LITERAL→CONSTANT_CHANGED`; `ALL_EQUAL→primitive-valid change of the lowest-ordinal non-literal participant while leaving peers`; `IF_AND_ONLY_IF→make the lowest antecedent true and consequent false`; `IMPLIES→antecedent true/consequent false`; `EXACTLY_ONE→populate the lowest absent second arm, or remove the highest present arm if the positive has none absent`; `SUBSET→append the primitive-minimal member not in the superset`; `ORDINAL_SUCCESSOR→increment successor once more`; `STATE_TRANSITION→replace destination with lexicographically first closed-enum state that is not an allowed edge`; `TYPED_REFERENCE→substitute the lexicographically first valid digest of a different catalog type`; `DIGEST_PREIMAGE→flip the first preimage byte while retaining the old digest`; `DERIVATION→change the lowest derived output using its primitive-valid change`; `AGGREGATE_LEQ→increase the lowest addend by exactly `limit-sum+1`. Dependent self-digests only are recomputed. A record-wide `$` predicate must name operands in normalized arguments. The generated candidate must remain canonical and valid for every primitive type, bound, enum, cardinality and reference-shape descriptor and must make the named target predicate false. Other cross-field predicates may deterministically become false when they logically entail or couple the target (for example a GET-only route predicate after an exact GET literal changes); the validator records the complete sorted false-predicate-ID set and still requires `PREDICATE_INVALID`. If the named target is not false or any descriptor constraint is false, extraction fails; B has no fallback choice or search.

The path/DDL identity seeds are concrete: positive `path="a"`, `absPath="/a"`, `authorityDomain="a"`, and `dbIdentity="aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa"`. Required single-constraint rejections include `path="/a"`, `absPath="a"`, `absPath="/a/../b"`, `authorityDomain="a/b"`, 63-hex `dbIdentity`, and uppercase-first-byte `dbIdentity`; their mutation codes are respectively `BAD_CHAR`, `BAD_CHAR`, `BAD_CHAR`, `BAD_CHAR`, `SHORT`, and `UPPERCASE`, with required errors `BOUND_INVALID`, `BOUND_INVALID`, `NON_CANONICAL`, `BOUND_INVALID`, `BOUND_INVALID`, and `BOUND_INVALID`. The catalog also instantiates those same authority-domain/db-identity positives through the exact PostgreSQL CHECK expressions; disagreement between wire and DDL acceptance is extraction failure.

The two existing concrete vectors remain frozen: WorkClassification digest `d79b2b9c6e77db88f096bfe5597ec56599369ee4d34cf3c23a64ae685fd5f684` for `{"kind":"WorkClassificationV1","schema_version":"work-classification-v1","classification":"CODE_BEARING","eligible_paths":[],"rationale":"ABCP assurance-governance enforcement changes authority-bearing code","classifier_identity":"controller"}`; FinalReviewProfile digest `073885532416375d9cb7e81d674812131eabfd1929867daa041490c854b49553` for `{"kind":"FinalReviewProfileV1","schema_version":"final-review-profile-v1","profile_id":"ABCP_XHIGH_FRESH_V1","provider":"codex","model":"gpt-5.6-sol","reasoning_effort":"xhigh","fresh_session":true,"required_critical":0,"required_major":0,"classification_vocabulary":["ASSURANCE_MODEL_GAP","IMPLEMENTATION_FINDING"]}`. The J-05 concrete component vectors/digests are frozen below.

The manifest contains exactly these sorted full paths: `docs/architecture/ASSURANCE_MODEL_AND_PROOF_OBLIGATION_POLICY.md`, `docs/architecture/AUTONOMOUS_EXECUTION_GOVERNANCE.md`, `docs/architecture/CONTEXT_AUTHORITY_AND_CAPSULE_POLICY.md`, `docs/architecture/DECISION_AND_ACCEPTANCE_POLICY.md`, `docs/architecture/IMPLEMENTATION_DESIGN_GATE.md`.

`proof_obligation_set_sha256`, `evidence_matrix_sha256`, `resource_profile_set_sha256`, and `canonical_vector_catalog_sha256` are independent content-addressed artifacts, not aliases for the whole assurance-model digest. A validates all four canonical projections from the frozen model. Every A checkpoint/A→B grant/B capsule, B or correction-B checkpoint/B→C grant/replacement-C capsule, and every C/integration/post-merge checkpoint preserves those four plus policy manifest, assurance model, semantic registry, work classification and final-review profile exactly. A missing/mismatched digest or required V1 grant floor is lineage invalidation, never a side lookup or inferred fallback.

Root sealing is an explicit acyclic bootstrap, not a digest fixed point. First seal the root A `ContextCapsuleV4` with no accepted-lineage field. Next compute `CheckpointGrantParentV2`, seal the initial A→B `StageGrantV2` with no accepted-lineage field, put that grant digest into and seal the full `DESIGN_ACCEPTED` checkpoint (also with no accepted-lineage field), preserving the exact current checkpoint-core→grant→full-checkpoint order. Then seal `AcceptedALineageV1` over those three final digests and install it in controller state. Every B/C/correction capsule, checkpoint and grant must carry that exact lineage digest. Placeholder/self-referential lineage digests are invalid.

The correction chain is singular and explicit: failed C publishes `FailedStageV1` and invalidates its exact C tip; controller CAS publishes one `CorrectionBGrantV1`; the correction-B `ContextCapsuleV4.PhaseAuthorityV4.parent` names the failed C capsule/checkpoint and that correction grant; correction B converges into a `PhaseCheckpointV2`; the replacement-C `StageGrantV2` and capsule name that checkpoint/grant and repeat the same `failed_stage_sha256`/`correction_grant_sha256`. All independent assurance digests and all original required blocker/invariant/operation/final-review floors stay byte-equal, while allowed paths, authorized findings and bounds may only narrow. This is still the same three-capsule stage model—correction B and replacement C are new generations of B and C, not a fourth capsule.

The external-effect authority dependency graph is also frozen and acyclic. Its only edges are `INTEGRATION_ACCEPTED decision/local tree observation → PRPublicationAuthorityV1 → PRLifecycleUpsertPayloadV1/PRLifecycleWriteAttemptV1 → PREffectRequestV1 → PR intent/barrier/claim/admission → indexed PR result or reconciliation → MergeReadinessAuthorityV1 → ReadyForMergeEvidenceV1 + exact READY ledger event → retained ReadyAuthorityBindingV1/ExpectedMergeContentV1/MergePolicyV1 → GitHubMergeAuthorityAdapterV1 → complete inherited GitHubLifecycleAuthorityV1 → TargetSubmissionV1/MergeEffectRequestV1 → merge chain`. No node before PR APPLIED contains a digest of READY evidence, READY binding, expected merge content, merge policy, adapter or complete inherited authority. The graph validator topologically sorts these named nodes and rejects any added reverse edge or digest cycle.

### Frozen semantic registry and final-review authority

Component scopes are frozen: `PF-GOV={internal/context/**,internal/governance/**,internal/authority/**,internal/ledger/**,cmd/abcp/**}`; `PF-BACKEND={internal/authoritybackend/**,cmd/abcp/**,go.mod,go.sum}`; `PF-RUN={internal/run/**,internal/acceptance/**,cmd/abcp/**}`; `PF-MERGE={internal/ledger/**,internal/githublifecycle/**,internal/mergelifecycle/**,cmd/abcp/**}`; `PF-PR={internal/ledger/**,internal/prlifecycle/**,cmd/abcp/**}`; `PF-READY={internal/integrationgate/**,internal/githublifecycle/**,internal/ledger/**,internal/governance/**,cmd/abcp/**}`; `PF-ACCEPT={scripts/acceptance/assurance-go-test-exact.py,scripts/acceptance/assurance-graph-validate.py,scripts/acceptance/assurance-lifecycle-integration.sh,scripts/acceptance/assurance-runner-preflight.sh}`; `PF-SECRET={scripts/acceptance/assurance-secret-scan.py,internal/run/**,internal/authoritybackend/**,cmd/abcp/**}`; `PF-RESOURCE={internal/run/**,internal/acceptance/**,internal/authoritybackend/**,scripts/acceptance/assurance-runner-preflight.sh,cmd/abcp/**}`.

Every runtime finding has a unique `finding_id`, exactly one frozen `rule_id=AG-PO-nnn`, classification and evidence digest; reviewer/model output is **never path authority**. The controller recomputes `changed_paths` from the exact accepted-B base→failed-candidate Git diff, expands the frozen semantic rule scope, intersects those with the original accepted-B allowed paths, and publishes `ControllerAffectedPathSetV1`. For implementation findings, `derived_allowed_paths` is the union of each validated finding's `derived_affected_paths`; every member must therefore be in `rule scope ∩ original B scope ∩ controller-recomputed changed paths`. Empty intersection, a required correction outside that intersection, an unmapped rule, or any model gap returns A. The bound final-review profile is the canonical `ABCP_XHIGH_FRESH_V1` vector above.

### Executable validity and drain-before-upgrade

`HistoricalValid(record)` validates immutable canonical bytes and full digest ancestor chain under the schema that created them. `OperationallyUsable(record,state,operation)` additionally requires same repository/controller/A lineage, exact current generation/tip, no invalidation/A-required flag, correct operation, and any existing time-bound lease not expired. Resource/evidence/accounting CAS revisions do **not** invalidate stage authority; only stage/effect transition/invalidation CAS changes stage generation/tip.

`V3Drained(v1,directory)` uses the exact existing V1 vocabulary and is true only when all of these hold under the cutover fence: strict-canonical `ControllerStateV1.Kind=="GovernanceControllerStateV1"`, its `ControllerIdentity`, `RepositoryIdentity` and decoded `Revision=r` equal the selected backend row; `ActiveInvocation==nil`; `FixBatchLeaseSHA256==""`; and the lease tuple is either `(Lease=nil,MutationState=nil)` or a structurally valid historical pair whose `MutationState.LeaseTipSHA256==Lease.LeaseSHA256`, `LeaseStatus==CONSUMED`, and receipt tip validates. Exact live statuses `ISSUED` and `CONSUMING` both fail. A partial lease/mutation pair, mismatched tip, active fix-batch reservation, or active invocation fails. In addition, either no V3 lineage ever started (`BCapsuleSHA256==""`, `CheckpointTip=nil`, `NextStageGrant=nil`, `Derivation=nil`) or the exact checkpoint tip validates as `POST_MERGE_ACCEPTED`; retained review/finding/consumed-lease/grant/derivation bytes are historical only at that terminal tip.

The directory predicate is global, not a fresh host's absence check. `PredecessorAuthorityDirectoryV1` must be sealed and contain every authority-bearing physical identity reachable by the sole authority-domain/controller credential: the workflow row, every bound `ledger.JSONLLedger` plus run ID, the process-startup `ABCP_PR_ADMISSION_ROOT` used by `internal/prlifecycle`, and every `internal/mergelifecycle.Config.StateRoot`. Each binding carries an immutable controller scan artifact. A ledger binding fails drain unless its exact run tip is one of the existing no-outgoing-transition states `{COMPLETED,FAILED,CANCELLED}`; any admitted `.run-locks` operation or `ActiveTransitionBarrier` also fails. A PR binding fails if any exact current V1 revision has `generation`/`submitted` without a valid terminal, or terminal disposition is `remote_diverged_after_write`/otherwise unresolved; success is only the existing `applied_confirmed` or `applied_reconciled`. A merge binding fails if any `target-submission` or target HTTP reservation lacks a validated `target-outcome`/`target-reconciliation` proving the exact existing `applied` or `not_applied` disposition, if `provider_accounting_pending` is true, if its ledger terminal is absent where `applied` requires it, or if any transition barrier is active. Existing `unknown` fails drain. Unknown files, roots, runs, identities or scan truncation fail `V3_DRAIN_REQUIRED`; no caller may assert the directory is complete.

Before cutover, trusted composition rotates/revokes legacy runtime credentials and requires every V1 workflow CAS, ledger transition, PR admission/provider call and merge admission/provider call to register an ACTIVE `PredecessorWriterLeaseV1` and hold the shared PostgreSQL cutover lock for `(authority_domain,controller_identity,writer_epoch)` across its complete state/store/barrier operation. Missing registration fails before mutation. Trusted composition must use `FencedWorkflowAuthorityBackendV1`, which wraps the exact previously selected `WorkflowAuthorityBackendV1`: every V1 CAS obtains the shared writer lease, verifies the directory remains `OPEN` at the bound epoch, performs exactly one underlying CAS, and releases the lease. The wrapped backend identity is immutable in the directory. After `TOMBSTONED`, the wrapper rejects before touching the old backend. This preparation strengthens live V3 admission but does not grandfather a V3 lineage after activation.

The only upgrade operation is one SERIALIZABLE PostgreSQL transaction. It takes the exclusive form of that cutover lock (therefore waiting for all admitted shared writers), locks the successor workflow and directory rows, requires no ACTIVE writer lease, rechecks every registered scan/barrier and `V3Drained`, then—while the exclusive fence still blocks every wrapped V1 CAS—calls the bound underlying V1 backend exactly once to load its current canonical bytes/revision and verifies its immutable backend identity. Those bytes are stable for the remainder of the exclusive fence even when the predecessor backend is not PostgreSQL. In that same PostgreSQL transaction it (1) create-or-verifies an `ArtifactBlobV1` containing those exact bytes, (2) inserts `PredecessorCutoverV1` and changes the directory to `TOMBSTONED`, and (3) replaces the workflow payload with strict `ControllerStateV2` at exactly `r+1`, referencing the exact V1 blob/revision/cutover and `GovernanceActivationV2`. The transaction commit is the single linearization point. Any earlier V1 CAS committed before the exclusive lock and is in the captured bytes; no later V1 CAS/provider admission can acquire the shared fence or pass the tombstone check. Crash before commit leaves V1 OPEN and unchanged; lost commit response is fresh-connection read-back of cutover/V2 exact digests only. Therefore exact V1 bytes are preserved before they cease to be operational, with no live V3 continuation and an exactly empty `grandfathered_v3`.

## Successor activation and immutable artifact authority

`GovernanceActivationV2` installs V4 only from exact existing merge-authorized + post-merge-accepted authority for the policy merge commit, exact installed predecessor activation, authenticated principal and protected base. The controller recomputes the exact five manifest files above. V4 activation additionally requires the directory/fence protocol, `V3Drained`, the same-transaction immutable predecessor artifact/cutover, and the PostgreSQL database-isolation bootstrap below.

Every digest referenced by V2 state/capsules/checkpoints/grants/model/evidence must resolve to its typed immutable blob plus any required occurrence in the same authenticated domain. Blob publication is create-or-verify on digest/size/exact bytes; occurrence publication is a distinct stage/subject/publisher identity. State CAS may reference only committed typed artifacts. A crash between blob commit and occurrence/state CAS leaves a harmless unreferenced blob, not a fabricated occurrence. Fresh hosts traverse ancestors exclusively by typed digests and the shared predecessor directory; host-local absence is never authority.

## Durable publication and remote-effect recovery matrix

| Family | Atomic authority boundary | Crash/ambiguity semantics |
|---|---|---|
| immutable blob/occurrence | blob create-or-verify; separate publisher transaction allocates stage ordinal and inserts occurrence | unreferenced blob is non-authoritative; committed occurrence is never inferred from blob reuse |
| publisher/stage close/inventory | open, append, finalize, abandon and close all lock stage then publisher; close proves zero ACTIVE rows, changes `OPEN→CLOSED`, and fixes `through_occurrence_ordinal` | crashed ACTIVE owner is exact-proof ABANDONED; close reports bounded active IDs for recovery and never waits while holding a DB lock; commit is the cutoff and later publisher mutation fails |
| stage/checkpoint/grant/evidence selection | expected-revision V2 CAS referencing durable artifact | ambiguous CAS is read-back/reconcile only; no second mutation until exact applied/not-applied proof |
| resource reservation | shared worker-row transaction plus workflow CAS charges the identical full vector and assigns ordinal | no process/container/daemon use before both commits; ambiguous cleanup is `RECOVERY_HOLD` and remains maximum-charged |
| failed stage / correction grant | failed-stage invalidation CAS, then separately derived correction-grant CAS | finding packet alone has zero mutation authority; grant paths are registry-derived |
| PR effect | exact integration event + pre-READY `PRPublicationAuthorityV1` + typed authentication/scope reads + one mutation credential → exact request/intent → typed V4 ledger barrier → PG claim/admission/index → provider | claim loss before admission is provable `NOT_APPLIED`; response and result+index pointer commit atomically; only an indexed-result-absent CAS creates the response-free anchor; exact APPLIED derives READY/event before PG publication settlement |
| merge effect | exact PR-derived READY + additive merge-authority adapter + typed authentication/scope reads + one mutation credential → exact TargetSubmission request/intent → typed V4 ledger barrier → PG claim/admission/index → provider | same result/index/first-reconciliation rule; only exact APPLIED authority can append MERGED before PG settlement; UNKNOWN retains the barrier |
| post-merge | exact terminal ledger event/evidence then V2 settlement CAS | ledger result remains factual run truth; failed acceptance emits `PostMergeFailureV1`, sets PG non-completion, no automatic rollback |

Raw effect tokens are generated from 256 bits of OS CSPRNG immediately before the winning claim CAS; only SHA-256(token) is durable. The provider adapter accepts both an in-memory `OneShotEffectCapability` and a non-copyable `BoundProviderCredentialCapability` holding the exact credential bytes represented by `ProviderCredentialUseV1`. `Take()` acquires the exact run-transition lease, verifies the raw effect token, effect-specific request, pre/post-READY authority as applicable, `ProviderAuthenticationContextV1`, mutation credential commitment/header commitment, current claim, decoded typed V4 barrier commitment, exact current source event and PG/index revision, then CAS-publishes `EffectCallAdmissionV1`; only a confirmed admission lets the adapter cross the transport boundary. Immediately before transport, the credential capability recomputes both commitments, requires equality with the request/admission/context and injects that same header; both capabilities atomically consume themselves before the lease is released. The typed identity/scope reads used to construct the context are performed through credential capabilities with their own recorded `ProviderCredentialUseV1`; for installation auth, the APP_JWT may prove `/app` identity but the exact INSTALLATION_TOKEN mutation capability must prove `/installation` and every paginated `/installation/repositories` page containing the target. No durable object contains reusable credential/header bytes. Another host can read commitments but cannot reconstruct either capability. An ambiguous admission CAS is read-back only: absent means no call, present means possible call.

The complete PR/merge attempt transition table is closed:

| From | Required durable facts | Only allowed action / linearization | To and crash recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| no attempt | current admissible ledger event/run/candidate and current PG stage tips | publish typed intent with next permitted ordinal; no provider access | `INTENT_DURABLE`; an orphan intent grants nothing |
| `INTENT_DURABLE` | acquire `ledger.AcquireRunTransition(run_id)`, rescan exact bound source event, construct typed `EffectBarrierCommitmentV1` and embedded `EffectLedgerBarrierV4` | ledger installs only that decoded typed barrier, then PG publishes `EffectTransitionBarrierV1`; release run lease only after both are confirmed | barrier-only crash: under run lease plus locked PG predecessor, create exact `EffectUnclaimedAbandonmentV1`, pass it through ledger `ResolveEffectBarrierV4`, read back absence and publish resolution; no claim/capability may coexist |
| barrier active | same PG predecessor revision; PR/C invalidation competes on that exact row | one PG CAS installs typed claim, sets coordination `PR_SUBMITTING`/`MERGE_SUBMITTING`, and returns raw-token capability only to winner | `CLAIMED_PRE_CALL`; lost CAS response is read-back, never a second token/claim |
| `CLAIMED_PRE_CALL` | exact claim/barrier/request, no call-admission | live winner may `Take()`; if winner is gone, exclusive PG read proves admission absent and publishes `EffectPreCallClosureV1` | admitted → `CALL_POSSIBLE`; lost winner → settlement `NOT_APPLIED`, then exact barrier resolve/read-back and resolution record |
| `CALL_POSSIBLE` | durable call-admission, attempt index and matching request/authentication/credential chain | at most one transport attempt; when any response exists, one SERIALIZABLE transaction create-or-verifies exact response/observation/result blobs, locks the state/index revision, publishes the successor index with `result_sha256`, and CASes state to that index | commit makes result fresh-process reachable; pre-commit crash rolls back every result pointer; lost commit response reloads state→index and never creates an anchor until the exact locked result slot is proved absent |
| `UNKNOWN` | exact indexed result or indexed response-free anchor, admission/claim/request/authentication/credential and active barrier | append bounded typed reconciliation at ordinal 1 from the indexed result/anchor, then gap-free `n-1→n`; each blob+index-tip+state update is one SERIALIZABLE CAS; zero mutation calls | observation may become `APPLIED` or `NOT_APPLIED`; still unknown CASes coordination to `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` and retains barrier |
| `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` | same ordinal/intention/admission/request and retained exact barrier | append only the next bounded read-only reconciliation; no new intent, capability or mutation call | decisive proof follows the corresponding APPLIED/NOT_APPLIED row; otherwise remain `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` |
| PR `APPLIED` | effect-specific authenticated observation exactly repeats principal/application, repository, PR identity/document and base/head refs/OIDs | build `ReadyForMergeEvidenceV1`; ledger `AppendOrVerifyEffectTransitionV4` validates exact authority and appends only `INTEGRATION_ACCEPTED→READY_FOR_MERGE`; reopen/verify event; PG settlement to `PR_PUBLISHED`; typed resolve/read-back | ledger-first crash settles PG from exact event; PG can never claim PR published without exact READY evidence/event |
| PR `NOT_APPLIED` | pre-call closure or provider proof that exact request did not apply | PG settlement CAS to `PR_NOT_APPLIED`, exact-resolve/read-back the barrier, then CAS-publish its resolution record | only after all three facts may `EffectReauthorizationV1` authorize ordinal `n+1` from a newly revalidated integration event/tip |
| merge `APPLIED` | effect-specific authenticated observation repeats principal/application, repository, PR, refs/OIDs and exact `TargetSubmissionV1`, with result pair | ledger `AppendOrVerifyEffectTransitionV4` validates exact authority and appends only `READY_FOR_MERGE→MERGED`; reopen/verify event; PG settlement to `MERGE_APPLIED`; typed resolve/read-back | ledger-first crash settles PG from exact event; PG can never claim APPLIED without that event |
| merge `NOT_APPLIED` | pre-call closure or authenticated provider proof | PG settlement CAS to `MERGE_NOT_APPLIED`, exact-resolve/read-back barrier and publish resolution | ledger remains READY; only `EffectReauthorizationV1` plus resolution and revalidated READY/tips permits ordinal `n+1` |
| settled `APPLIED` or `NOT_APPLIED` | exact byte-identical terminal settlement | create-or-verify/read only | conflicting result, request digest, ordinal, winner or settlement is integrity failure; APPLIED always wins over cancellation |

Within one attempt, `provider_request_sha256`, `authentication_context_sha256` and `mutation_credential_use_sha256` are byte-identical—not merely semantically equivalent—in the effect-specific request, intent, typed barrier commitment where named, claim, call admission, attempt index, transport record, every result/reconciliation and APPLIED observation; settlement/resolution reach the same three values through their indexed winning outcome and request. Reauthorization repeats the prior/new typed requests. The generic `ProviderRequestV1` is only the exact raw transport child and grants no authority by itself. A result/reconciliation artifact cannot settle a different principal, app/installation, repository scope, credential, document, PR, ref, request, run, ledger event, claim or ordinal. A new ordinal never revives either old capability and is forbidden from `APPLIED`, `UNKNOWN` or `RECOVERY_REQUIRED`.

The result/anchor crash partition is exhaustive. (1) A crash before the result/index/state transaction commits leaves no authoritative result pointer; any harmless prewritten content blob is unreachable and cannot be interpreted. (2) A committed transaction, including a lost commit response, makes `ControllerStateV2→EffectAttemptIndexV1.result_sha256→typed result/observation/response` the only recovery path. (3) An anchor transaction locks that same state and index revision and succeeds only when `result_sha256`, `reconciliation_anchor_sha256` and `reconciliation_tip_sha256` are all absent; it create-or-verifies the anchor and publishes its index/state pointers atomically. Thus result and anchor are competing expected-revision CAS winners and can never coexist. (4) A crash after either pointer but before reconciliation/ledger/settlement reloads the winner and continues read-only. No filesystem scan, process memory, provider-response inference or unindexed blob lookup is authority.

Barrier removal has one frozen cross-store recovery order. The resolver holds `AcquireRunTransition` and the locked PG effect row, builds `EffectBarrierRemovalProofV1` from either an exact same-chain `RESOLVE` settlement or exact `EffectUnclaimedAbandonmentV1`, and calls only `ledger.ResolveEffectBarrierV4(barrier,proof,coordinationVerifier)`. The ledger boundary decodes the embedded commitment, validates the typed proof and exact current source/authorized APPLIED terminal event as applicable, rejects any competing FAILED/CANCELLED/other terminal, removes only that barrier, then the controller reopens the ledger to prove absence and commits `EffectBarrierResolutionV1`. Ledger deletion is the removal linearization point. Crash before it leaves the barrier active; crash after deletion but before the PG record can only repeat absence verification and the same resolution CAS. The PG row lock excludes a new claim during unclaimed removal, and provider admission independently requires the active typed barrier plus exact source event under the run lease.

At the ledger mutation boundary, an active `EffectLedgerBarrierV4` makes existing `Append`, `AppendOrVerify`, `AppendOrVerifyLeased`, legacy `AppendOrVerifyTransition` and legacy `ResolveTransitionBarrier` all fail closed regardless of any supplied digest or terminal state. Only `AppendOrVerifyEffectTransitionV4(event,authority)` may append, and it independently re-parses the current source line, barrier, commitment, outcome and deterministic event bytes while holding the run lock. Only `ResolveEffectBarrierV4` may remove. Thus FAILED, CANCELLED, a different APPLIED event, a byte-valid event from a different run/ordinal/kind and the legacy “any terminal” path cannot cross an active V4 barrier.

## Frozen resource authority

Validator authority—not the caller—binds exactly one direct `MaterializedResourceProfileV1` digest containing the flattened process/output/cache/container/image/daemon/secret/network vector used by that execution. Component names are derivation inputs only; no validator selects components at reservation time. Ordinary roots and every depth-1 child have direct=allocation. The sole J-05 root additionally binds the exact larger `DOGFOOD_POSTGRES_MATERIALIZED_V1` allocation envelope while its execution binds `DOGFOOD_DRIVER_DIRECT_MATERIALIZED_V1`; those two named digests and counters are both immutable in its worker/workflow reservations. No “equal or stronger” substitution exists. The shared worker coordinator locks one immutable `dbIdentity` worker row and applies root/suballocation predicates across domains/controllers/lineages. Direct reservation DML is revoked. Workflow CAS may reference a committed shared reservation only; a failed CAS leaves `RECOVERY_HOLD` fully charged.

Worker capacity predicates are checked-u64 inequalities over the unprefixed allocation counters of every non-RELEASED **root only**: `Σ(process_memory+container_memory+unique_daemon_memory)≤12884901888`; `Σ(process_pids+container_pids+unique_daemon_pids)≤384`; `Σ(process_cpu+container_cpu+unique_daemon_cpu)≤600000`, every nonzero period `100000`; `Σ(nofile)≤4096`; `Σ(cache+container_image+unique_daemon_cache)≤4294967296`; `Σ(container_tmpfs)≤2147483648`; `Σ(container_shm)≤268435456`; `Σ(container_log+unique_daemon_log)≤67108864`; `Σ(container_image)≤2147483648`. Child rows never enter these worker sums. Capacity bytes/digest are strict-validated; a host value cannot replace them. “Unique daemon” remains one locked/refcounted `(daemon_capacity_sha256,image_digest,docker_root_identity)` charge. J-05 root allocation remains exactly CPU `300000+200000+100000=600000` and memory 6 GiB combined. Devagent preflight still requires ≥8 CPUs/≥16 GiB. All active/hold lifecycles count at maxima; only atomic RELEASED subtracts.

Nesting is representable inclusive suballocation. A root alone charges its immutable allocation envelope. Under the same worker lock, first child creation freezes `children_frozen_at_revision`; for every one of the 20 reservation dimensions, checked `parent.direct_dimension + Σ(non-RELEASED child.allocation_dimension) ≤ parent.allocation_dimension`. Period fields are either zero or identical 100000, never summed. Each child names parent digest+frozen revision, has allocation=direct, adds zero worker charge, and cannot parent. Siblings consume the same remainder. Parent allocation/direct/profile/revision cannot grow after freezing; release is forbidden while a child is non-RELEASED. Child worker/domain/lineage/delegation identities are exact. Evidence actuals must be ≤ each row's direct allocation and reconcile into parent inclusive usage. Restart/controller/workflow/domain changes cannot obtain another envelope.

The exact profile values are:

| Profile | Process/output/cache | Container | Docker daemon |
|---|---|---|---|
| `NORMAL_V1` | memory 2 GiB; PIDs 64; CPU `200000/100000`; NOFILE 256; wall 1,800,000 ms; stdout/stderr 16 MiB each; evidence 32 MiB; transient 512 MiB; cache 512 MiB | all zero; no image; no container | all zero |
| `SECRET_CAPABLE_V1` | same process/NOFILE/wall/evidence/transient/cache as NORMAL; stdout/stderr retained bytes exactly 0 | component fields all zero | component fields all zero |
| `DOGFOOD_V1` | memory 4 GiB; PIDs 128; CPU `300000/100000`; NOFILE 512; wall 5,400,000 ms; stdout/stderr retained bytes 0; evidence 128 MiB; transient 2 GiB; cache 1 GiB; capability CODEX_PROVIDER; network LOOPBACK_BROKER | component fields all zero | component fields all zero |
